      "passage": "Roads connect human communities, but they can divide animal habitats. When animals need to reach food, shelter, or seasonal routes on the other side, crossing traffic can be dangerous. A wildlife crossing is a bridge or tunnel designed to offer another route.\nBuilding a crossing alone does not guarantee success. Planners first study where animals already move. A bridge in a rarely used location may attract few animals, even if it is well built. Different species also need different designs: a small animal may use a sheltered tunnel, while a larger animal may need a broad open route.\nFences can guide animals toward a crossing rather than the road. Native plants can provide cover near an entrance. After construction, researchers monitor animal use to learn whether the design is working. If monitoring reveals a problem, planners can adjust the approach. Effective crossings depend on research before construction and continued learning afterward.",
